4277 B8CE C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
4278 B8D0 0450  20 	b *r0
4279               	;
4280               	; 	' ======================================================================
4281               	; 	' DRAWING THE STORE -- four blits, once per screen crossing
4282               	; 	' ======================================================================
4283               	; 	' This is the whole cost of the flip, and it is paid on a discrete event
4284               	; 	' rather than every frame. RallyX's pan repaints 576 chars every time the
4285               	; 	' camera moves one cell; this repaints 640 about once every two seconds
4286               	; 	' of running.
4287               	; 	' SPRITES OFF BEFORE THE BLIT, NOT AFTER IT. This routine WAITs between
4288               	; 	' bands -- one band a frame, because a burst of VDP writes past a few
4289               	; 	' dozen in one frame is silently dropped -- so painting the new screen
4290               	; 	' takes several frames, and for all of them the OLD screen's actors and
4291               	; 	' obstacles were still sitting on top of it. Following Harry through a
4292               	; 	' seam showed him and every cart from the floor he had just left,
4293               	; 	' standing on a shop that was assembling itself underneath them.
4294               	; 	'
4295               	; 	' They are put back by the normal draw on the same pass, so the crossing
4296               	; 	' reads as a cut rather than as a dissolve with ghosts in it.
4297               	; 	' EACH BAND IS FINISHED BEFORE THE FRAME IT IS DRAWN IN ENDS, and the
4298               	; 	' bands go TOP DOWN. Both halves of that matter.
4299               	; 	'
4300               	; 	' The blit paints the template -- shelves, pillars and all -- and the
4301               	; 	' radios and prizes then take out the fixtures they stand in. Those two
4302               	; 	' used to be four frames apart: all four bands blitted with a WAIT
4303               	; 	' between them, and only afterwards the clearing. So every screen change
4304               	; 	' showed its counters and beams for several frames and then erased them,
4305               	; 	' which reads as the shop flickering rather than as it being drawn.
4306               	; 	'
4307               	; 	' TOP DOWN because a band's beam tops are stamped into the SLAB ROW OF
4308               	; 	' THE BAND ABOVE. Going bottom-up, band 1's blit would paint over the
4309               	; 	' beam tops band 0 had just put there. Going top-down the band above is
4310               	; 	' always already drawn, so everything a band needs -- its own cells and
4311               	; 	' the row it borrows overhead -- is final by the time its WAIT arrives.
4312               	; 	'
4313               	; 	' `3 - dq` rather than `FOR dlv = 3 TO 0 STEP -1`: dlv is an unsigned
4314               	; 	' byte, so counting down past zero wraps to 255 and the loop never ends
4315               	; 	' (CLAUDE.md 3A).
4316               	; draw_screen:
4317               cvb_DRAW_SCREEN

4328               	; 		' WAIT FIRST, NOT LAST. A band is blitted and then corrected -- the
4329               	; 		' beam top stamped on, the pillar under a radio taken off -- and the
4330               	; 		' raster does not stop while that happens. Starting the burst in the
4331               	; 		' middle of a frame let the raster pass the band between the blit and
4332               	; 		' the correction, so the uncorrected version was shown for a frame:
4333               	; 		' at round start, after CLS, that reads as a support beam appearing
4334               	; 		' and being rubbed out. Beginning at vblank gives the whole burst a
4335               	; 		' clear run at the rows before the beam reaches them. It also still
4336               	; 		' gives each band its own frame, which is what stops a VDP write
4337               	; 		' burst past a few dozen from being silently dropped.

4426               	;
4427               	; 	' A SUPPORT BEAM HAS TO REACH THE FLOOR IT HOLDS UP, AND THAT FLOOR IS
4428               	; 	' NOT IN ITS OWN BAND. A beam fills its band's four air rows, so its top
4429               	; 	' pixel sits under the slab row of the band ABOVE -- and a slab row is
4430               	; 	' five pixels of bar over three of green (see SLAB), so the beam stopped
4431               	; 	' three pixels short of the bar on every storey. It read as a beam that
4432               	; 	' misses the floor, which is exactly what it was.
4433               	; 	'
4434               	; 	' It cannot be drawn into either template. The beam belongs to the band
4435               	; 	' below and the row belongs to the band above, and the two templates are
4436               	; 	' chosen independently per screen, so no template knows both. So the
4437               	; 	' band blits go down first and the tops are stamped afterwards, from a
4438               	; 	' per-template column table -- the same shape as the escalator's head cap.
4439               	; 	'
4440               	; 	' Bands 0 and 1 only. Band 2's top row is the ROOF DECK, which is grey
4441               	; 	' over grey with no green to bridge, and the roof itself has no band
4442               	; 	' above it at all.
4443               	; 	' BANDS 0 TO 2 -- ALL THREE SHOPPING FLOORS. It used to stop at 1,
4444               	; 	' because band 2's ceiling is the ROOF DECK and the deck had no green
4445               	; 	' under it: a beam there already ran straight into grey and needed
4446               	; 	' nothing. Giving the roof its three green rows (so the lift shaft stops
4447               	; 	' under it, like every other floor) took that away, and the top floor's
4448               	; 	' beams started falling three pixels short.
4449               	; 	'
4450               	; 	' So the roof gets its own version of the stamp. A beam HOLDS THE ROOF
4451               	; 	' UP and must reach it; the shaft merely stops beneath it.
4452               	; beam_one:
4453               cvb_BEAM_ONE

4987               	; 			' NO SPRITE HAZARD ON THE FLOOR THE CROOK IS STANDING ON,
4988               	; 			' AND THE DECISION IS MADE HERE -- once, as the screen is
4989               	; 			' drawn, and never revisited while it is on screen.
4990               	; 			'
4991               	; 			' It was a test in the draw loop and another in the
4992               	; 			' collision test, both re-evaluated every frame, so the
4993               	; 			' hazards came and went as Harry walked on and off the
4994               	; 			' screen: run him to the edge and a floor's worth of balls
4995               	; 			' appeared out of nothing behind him. A rule about what
4996               	; 			' this screen CONTAINS cannot be a per-frame question --
4997               	; 			' the answer changes while the player is looking at it.
4998               	; 			'
4999               	; 			' Zeroing the kind here also makes both of those tests
5000               	; 			' unnecessary: an obstacle that was never loaded cannot be
5001               	; 			' drawn and cannot hit anybody.
5002               	; 			'
5003               	; 			' Radios are exempt. They are characters rather than
5004               	; 			' sprites, they do not move, and a fixture the crook runs
5005               	; 			' past is not what this rule is about.

5167 BF60 D401  30 	movb r1,*r0
5168               	; 			' THE BALL IS ON THE GROUND WHEN HE GETS TO IT.
5169               	; 			'
5170               	; 			' The bounce phase used to be `lx AND 31` -- a byte out of
5171               	; 			' the placement table, which is to say arbitrary. Run onto
5172               	; 			' a screen and the first ball might be at the bottom of its
5173               	; 			' arc, where a jump clears it, or at the top, where it has
5174               	; 			' to be ducked, and nothing on the way in told you which.
5175               	; 			' Sometimes you could keep running and jump it; sometimes
5176               	; 			' the same approach at the same speed could not be jumped
5177               	; 			' at all. That is not difficulty, it is a coin toss.
5178               	; 			'
5179               	; 			' So the phase is worked BACKWARDS from the meeting. He
5180               	; 			' closes 4 px a pass and the ball 2, so they meet in
5181               	; 			' (gap / 6) passes; the phase advances one a pass and wraps
5182               	; 			' at 32, so seeding it with the NEGATIVE of that lands it on
5183               	; 			' phase 0 -- the ground -- exactly as they arrive.
5184               	; 			'
5185               	; 			' The gap is the same either way round: he enters at one
5186               	; 			' wall and the ball starts `stag` in from the other, so it
5187               	; 			' is 232 - stag whichever direction the floor runs.
5188               	; 			'
5189               	; 			' It stays forgiving rather than exact. The arc is flat near
5190               	; 			' the bottom, so anything within eight passes either side is
5191               	; 			' still under the jump; and it only holds for an unbroken
5192               	; 			' run, which is the case the player is entitled to read off
5193               	; 			' the screen. Duck, stop or take a hit and everything after
5194               	; 			' is out of step again -- which is the game.
5195               	; 			obp(li) = lx AND 31
5196 BF62 D020  34 	movb @cvb_LX,r0
     BF64 21CC     
5197 BF66 0201  20 	li r1,7936   ; 31*256
     BF68 1F00

5850               	;
5851               	; 	' ONE CELL OF THE DOORWAY, from its column (ccl 0-3), its band row
5852               	; 	' (crw 0-3) and the door state (cst). Four columns and four rows, so
5853               	; 	' sixteen cells, and the three interesting things about a cell are all
5854               	; 	' edges: column 0 and column 3 carry the JAMB, row 0 carries the LINTEL
5855               	; 	' and row 3 the SILL.
5856               	; 	'
5857               	; 	' THE SILL IS REVEALED AS THE DOORS PART, which falls out of this rather
5858               	; 	' than being arranged: shut, every cell is plain door and there is no
5859               	; 	' sill to see; part-open, only the middle two columns show car and sill;
5860               	; 	' open, all four do. A sill sitting under a shut door would be a ledge
5861               	; 	' with nothing behind it.
5862               	; 	'
5863               	; 	' Nested IFs, never `ccl > 0 AND ccl < 3` -- the 9900 backend miscompiles
5864               	; 	' a compare-AND-compare (CLAUDE.md 3A).
5865               	; car_cell:

5872               	; 	' part-open one -- which the cst=1 branch below never touches. The step
5873               	; 	' is then continuous across all four columns of the frame on every

6140               	; 		' HE IS STANDING ON A STEP, so he moves at the STEP's rate: 2 px
6141               	; 		' across and 1 up PER PASS -- the staircase's own slope, and exactly
6142               	; 		' what one phase of esc_tick moves the pattern by.
6143               	; 		'
6144               	; 		' NOT `fdv`, WHICH IS THE WHOLE POINT. Everything else in this loop
6145               	; 		' is paced by the frame delta, and pacing him that way too made him
6146               	; 		' outrun the steps the moment a pass cost more than one frame: his
6147               	; 		' feet started planted on a tread and ended floating above it.
6148               	; 		' Pacing the ANIMATION by fdv instead is worse -- four phases cannot
6149               	; 		' be stepped by 2 or 3 without aliasing, and the staircase runs
6150               	; 		' backwards (see esc_tick). The only arrangement that cannot drift
6151               	; 		' is the one where both are driven by the same fixed step, so the
6152               	; 		' rider is clocked by the pass, like the steps he is standing on.
6153               	; 		'
6154               	; 		' THE FIRST FEW PASSES ARE THE STEP ON. Walking on, his feet are on
6155               	; 		' the floor and the bottom tread is 4 to 7 px above them, so he
6156               	; 		' climbs at 2 px a pass until he catches it (the tread is rising at
6157               	; 		' 1, so the gap closes at 1 a pass) and then rides with it. Jumping
6158               	; 		' on he lands ON a tread, so eson is 0 and this never runs.
6159               	; 		IF esy < eson THEN esy = esy + 1

6461               	;
6462               	; 	' --- running
6463               	; 	' THE END WALLS ARE MOVEMENT LIMITS, NOT A CLAMP APPLIED AFTERWARDS.
6464               	; 	'
6465               	; 	' They used to be the latter, and that is the whole bug: on screen 7 Kelly
6466               	; 	' walked on to x = 254, the crossing routine then noticed there was no
6467               	; 	' screen 8 and set him to 232 -- a 22 px snap BACKWARDS. He reached the
6468               	; 	' wall and was thrown off it. Testing the limit BEFORE the step means he
6469               	; 	' arrives at the wall and stays there, which is what a wall does.
6470               	; 	' IN THE AIR HE IS BALLISTIC. The horizontal direction is fixed at
6471               	; 	' take-off and the stick is ignored until he lands: steering in mid-air
6472               	; 	' is not a jump, it is flight, and it also let him flip which way he was
6473               	; 	' facing halfway through an arc. kjdx is 0 for a standing jump, 1 for a
6474               	; 	' running one to the right, 2 to the left.
6475               	; 	IF klst = ST_JUMP THEN

6789               	;
6790               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------------------ escalator
6791               	; 	' ONE working escalator per floor, at that floor's alternating end --
6792               	; 	' west, east, west. An escalator drawn where none works would be a step
6793               	; 	' the player runs to and nothing happens.
6794               	; 	' ESCALATORS RUN BOTH WAYS. Stand at the foot and you are carried up;
6795               	; 	' stand at the head of the flight that arrives from the floor below and
6796               	; 	' you are carried down. It is one staircase used from either end, not two
6797               	; 	' -- which is also why the down zone on a floor is the UP zone of the
6798               	; 	' floor beneath it, offset by one band.
6799               	; 	' UP ONLY. Riding down was built at one point and has been taken out
6800               	; 	' again at the reviewer's direction -- these only ever climb.
6801               	; 	'
6802               	; 	' Losing it also removed the re-board lock, which existed solely because
6803               	; 	' stepping off at the head put you inside the zone that rode the same
6804               	; 	' flight back down. With no down ride there is no such zone: a floor's
6805               	; 	' escalator is at the opposite end from the one below it, so arriving at
6806               	; 	' the top of one never lands you on the foot of another.
6807               	; try_esc:

7365               	;
7366               	; 	' THE VICTROLA, AS CHARACTERS. It never moves, so a sprite spent a
7367               	; 	' scan-line slot on something that can be painted once when the screen is
7368               	; 	' drawn -- and at one 16x16 sprite it could only ever be one colour and
7369               	; 	' one size. Four cells is twice the area for no sprite at all.
7370               	; 	'
7371               	; 	' It stands ON the slab, so it fills the band's rows 2 and 3; row 4 is
7372               	; 	' the floor bar itself and draw_prizes uses row 3 for a collectible,
7373               	; 	' which is why the two never share a screen (genstore keeps prizes off
7374               	; 	' the screens that carry hazards).
7375               	; 	'
7376               	; 	' rdall = 1 paints all four cells, 0 repaints only the top-right one --
7377               	; 	' the pulse. Same address arithmetic either way, so the two cannot drift.
7378               	; 	' A BEAM TOP HANGS OUT OF THE FLOOR ABOVE, and the beam it belongs to has
7379               	; 	' just been taken away. `beam_tops` stamps SLABP -- the floor bar with the
7380               	; 	' support carried up through its lower three pixel rows -- into the slab
7381               	; 	' row above every pillar, and it runs BEFORE the radios and prizes are
7382               	; 	' placed. Clearing a pillar's own cells therefore left three grey pixels
7383               	; 	' dangling from the ceiling with nothing under them.
7384               	; 	'
7385               	; 	' TESTED, NOT ASSUMED: only SLABP is put back to SLAB. The row above the
7386               	; 	' top shopping floor is the ROOF DECK, a different character altogether,
7387               	; 	' and writing a floor bar over it would punch a hole in the roof.
7388               	; 	' A SHELF RUN IS FIVE CELLS WIDE and a radio covers two, so one standing
7389               	; 	' on a counter punched a hole through its middle and left the ends either
7390               	; 	' side -- the same incoherence the dangling pillar had, and worse, because
7391               	; 	' a counter reads as one object. The whole unit goes: walk outward along
7392               	; 	' the band's row 2 while the cell is still a shelf top, clearing it and
7393               	; 	' its bottom half together.
7394               	; 	'
7395               	; 	' Bounded at eight steps. The runs are five, so eight is slack rather
7396               	; 	' than a guess -- and an unbounded walk would run off the end of the row
7397               	; 	' if the name table ever held something unexpected.
7398               	; wipe_shelf:
7399               cvb_WIPE_SHELF
7400               	; 	wsn = 8

7472               	; 	' A PILLAR'S CAP MAY BE CLEARED; AN END WALL'S MAY NOT. beam_one stamps
7473               	; 	' both into the slab row of the band above, but they mean different
7474               	; 	' things. SLABP is the top of a free-standing pillar: a radio or a prize
7475               	; 	' stands in front of the pillar, so leaving its cap up there hangs a grey
7476               	; 	' stub over the fixture and that is what this routine exists to remove.
7477               	; 	' SLABE and ROOFSE are written ONLY at column 0 and column 31 -- read
7478               	; 	' beam_one, they are the two edge cases -- and there they are the

7480               	; 	' above. Clearing those took the support out from under the storey above
7481               	; 	' at the very edge of the screen, so the second floor visibly rested on
7482               	; 	' nothing.
7483               	; 	'
7484               	; 	' It could only ever show on the ESCALATOR screens, which is exactly how

7547               	; 	' used to be an unconditional green VPOKE, which is only correct when the

7908               	; 				' THEY WRAP. They do not turn round at the wall.
7909               	; 				' A bouncing obstacle is a pendulum: it has a near

8471               	;
8472               	; 	' THE QUARTER-PIXEL ACCUMULATOR. hx is one unsigned byte per screen (2),
8473               	; 	' so there is nowhere to keep a fraction -- the fraction lives here and
8474               	; 	' is spent as whole pixels. hsp4 = 6 walks 1,2,1,2 (1.5 px/frame); 7
8475               	; 	' walks 1,2,2,2 over four frames (1.75); 8 is a flat 2. Two IFs, not a
8476               	; 	' loop and not a divide: hacc is under 4 on entry and hsp4 is at most 8,
8477               	; 	' so it can never need a third. `%` and `/` both compile to a real
8478               	; 	' TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A) and this runs every frame.
8479               	; 	'
8480               	; 	' 7, NOT 6, AND THE REASON IS MEASURED. Tracked off the reference video
8481               	; 	' (DESIGN.md 4a), the 2600's Kelly covers 0.40 screen widths a second and
8482               	; 	' its Harry 0.193 -- a ratio of 2.07. At hsp4 = 6 ours was 2.67, and the
8483               	; 	' crook visibly strolled. 7 brings it to 2.29. 8 would match the original
8484               	; 	' exactly and cannot be used: Kelly's route here is 1.95x Harry's, so a
8485               	; 	' speed ratio of 2.0 leaves the on-foot chase 0.2 s of slack, which is no
8486               	; 	' chase at all. checkchase.py holds the line.
8487               	; 	' STILL RUNNING FROM THE STAIRS -- and this has to come BEFORE the move,
8488               	; 	' not after it. It sat below the two `IF hmv` blocks, where all it could
8489               	; 	' still reach was the animation counter: he faced the right way, played
8490               	; 	' the run cycle and did not travel a pixel. He stood at the foot of the
8491               	; 	' flight looking busy.
8492               	; 	'
8493               	; 	' TWO THINGS END THE RUN, AND BOTH ARE VISIBLE ON SCREEN.
8494               	; 	'
8495               	; 	' THE KOP ARRIVING ON THIS FLOOR OUTRANKS IT. This used to override the
8496               	; 	' flee as well as the climb, so for the whole of the run he held one
8497               	; 	' heading no matter where Kelly was -- and ran straight into him without
8498               	; 	' appearing to notice he was there. The flee above has already worked out
8499               	; 	' which way is away; clearing the run simply lets it stand.
8500               	; 	'
8501               	; 	' AND REACHING THE END WALL ENDS IT. It used to be a count of passes,
8502               	; 	' which expired somewhere in the middle of a floor and turned him round
8503               	; 	' for no reason the player could see. Running until the wall means every
8504               	; 	' change of direction has something on screen behind it.
8505               	; 	IF hrun > 0 THEN
8506 D866 D020  34 	movb @cvb_HRUN,r0
     D868 21A8

     D86C 0100

9047               	;
9048               	; 	' ======================================================================
9049               	; 	' COLLISIONS -- pure arithmetic against RAM, no VDP reads
9050               	; 	' ======================================================================
9051               	; 	' Kelly's box is [kfh, kfh + kh) above the slab; an obstacle's is
9052               	; 	' [ohb, oht). The ball's inset hitbox is what makes 8..10 avoidable both
9053               	; 	' ways -- without it there is a height at which the ball can be neither
9054               	; 	' jumped nor ducked, and an unavoidable hazard is not difficulty.
9055               	; 	' ONE HIT PER CONTACT, WITH A REFRACTORY PERIOD.
9056               	; 	'
9057               	; 	' The overlap test is true on every frame the player is touching
9058               	; 	' something, so with no latch at all one clumsy cart charges nine seconds
9059               	; 	' several times over -- and what the player sees is the clock jumping by
9060               	; 	' 27, from which the only available conclusion is that the penalty is
9061               	; 	' broken.
9062               	; 	'
9063               	; 	' A latch that clears on the first non-overlapping frame is still not
9064               	; 	' enough, and the case that breaks it is the BOUNCING BALL: it rises off
9065               	; 	' the player between bounces, which is a genuine loss of contact, so a
9066               	; 	' player standing still under one is taxed on every single bounce while
9067               	; 	' being given no opportunity to do anything about it. Technically two
9068               	; 	' collisions; in play, one situation.
9069               	; 	'
9070               	; 	' So obht() is a countdown, not a flag. A hit sets it to HITREF; it ticks
9071               	; 	' down only while the player is CLEAR, and the same obstacle cannot charge
9072               	; 	' again until it reaches zero. Contact holds the count up, so parking
9073               	; 	' inside something never earns a second penalty either.
9074               	; coll_obst:
9075               cvb_COLL_OBST
9076               	; 	IF klst = ST_ESC THEN RETURN		' riding is invincible, per the
9077 DCA2 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLST,r0
     DCA4 2190

     DCA8 0300

9611               	; 	' NO +8 ON EITHER SIDE, AND THAT IS THE FIX RATHER THAN A TIDY-UP. Both
9612               	; 	' centres are the left edge plus eight, so the eight cancels in the
9613               	; 	' difference -- but adding it first OVERFLOWED THE BYTE. Harry walks to
9614               	; 	' x 253 before he crosses a seam, and 253 + 8 wraps to 5, so a Kop
9615               	; 	' standing at the far LEFT of the screen measured five pixels to a crook
9616               	; 	' walking off the far RIGHT and arrested him across the whole store.
9617               	; 	'
9618               	; 	' Nothing warned: both are plain 8-bit variables, the wrap is silent, and
9619               	; 	' the catch it produces looks like a generous hitbox rather than like
9620               	; 	' arithmetic. The obstacle test has the same shape but cannot reach it --
9621               	; 	' obx tops out at 240 and klx at XWALL, so neither sum passes 255.

9684               	; 	' KELLY IS SPRITES 0, 1 AND 2 and nothing else ever is -- the VDP drops
9685               	; 	' the highest-numbered sprites on an over-full scanline, so the lowest
9686               	; 	' slots are the ones that can never disappear, and the player is the one
9687               	; 	' thing that must never disappear.
9688               	; 	'
9689               	; 	' THE BAND SPRITES SHARE A y ON PURPOSE. Slots 0 and 1 both sit at ky and
9690               	; 	' between them cover rows 0-15; slot 2 sits at ky+16. Boxes 0/1 and box 2
9691               	; 	' never share a scanline, so an actor costs at most TWO boxes on any line
9692               	; 	' -- and the VDP counts boxes, not pixels, so an empty overlap would have
9693               	; 	' cost just as much as a full one. Two actors meeting is four, exactly
9694               	; 	' the per-line limit, which is why obstacles are suppressed on Harry's
9695               	; 	' floor rather than merely being a kindness.

9840               	; 		' FOUR RUN FRAMES FROM TWO BITS of the animation counter, and no
9841               	; 		' divide -- `/` compiles to a real TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A) and
9842               	; 		' this is per-frame code. Two adjacent bits give 0,4,8,12.
9843               	; 		'

10130               	; 	' HIS ARMS SWING, on the same bit of the animation counter that picks the
10131               	; 	' legs -- so the arm that is forward is the one opposite the leading leg,
10132               	; 	' which is what running looks like.
10133               	; 	' THE ARMS SWING ONCE PER FULL LEG CYCLE, ON BIT 16 -- not on bit 8.
10134               	; 	'
10135               	; 	' Bit 8 is one BEAT, and the legs take four of them (bits 8 and 16). So an
10136               	; 	' arm swing keyed to bit 8 completed twice for every one pass of the legs:
10137               	; 	' the top half of him flapping at double the speed of the bottom half.
10138               	; 	' That is the jig. It is not a drawing problem and no amount of redrawing
10139               	; 	' the legs would have touched it.
10140               	; 	'
10141               	; 	' On bit 16 the arms hold through beats 1-2 and swap for 3-4, which is
10142               	; 	' also the right PHASE: beat 1 leads with one leg and beat 3 with the
10143               	; 	' other, so the arm opposite the leading leg is forward in each -- which
10144               	; 	' is what running looks like.
10145               	; 	' THE TORSO RUNS ON THE SAME TWO BITS AS THE LEGS. With four drawn poses
10146               	; 	' the shoulders and arms swing WITH the stride; the old two-frame body had
10147               	; 	' a clock of its own, which is what read as a flap rather than a run.
10148               	; 	hp = P_HBODY

10189               	; 	' half a stride behind his shirt. Same reason hqs comes from hq.

10224               	; 	' HE IS STRIPED FROM CAP TO HEM. Both stripe colours run the whole upper
10225               	; 	' half, so both boxes span rows 0-15 and neither can be tucked away --
10226               	; 	' affordable only because Kelly's hat moved into a box of its own. His
10227               	; 	' FACE is pushed DOWN instead (hy+3, box 3..18) so it clears the cap
10228               	; 	' rows, where his two stripe boxes plus Kelly's hat and face already
10229               	; 	' make four. Worst line of a meeting: exactly four, nothing dropped.
10230               	; 	' HE RISES A PIXEL ON THE RECOVERY BEATS. A runner is highest at mid-flight
10231               	; 	' and lowest at contact; with the body pinned at a constant y, alternating
10232               	; 	' legs under a motionless torso reads as a dance rather than as travel.
10233               	; 	'
10234               	; 	' One pixel is enough at this size, and it lifts the BODY ONLY -- the legs
10235               	; 	' keep their y, so the planted foot stays on the floor and the figure
10236               	; 	' stretches at the waist instead of hopping. Lifting the legs too would be
10237               	; 	' a jump.
10238               	; 	'
10239               	; 	' Beats 2 and 4 are the recoveries, which is exactly `hanim AND 8` -- the
10240               	; 	' cycle's LOW beat bit. IT MUST MOVE WITH THE BEAT BITS ABOVE IT: on the
10241               	; 	' wrong bit he rises and falls twice per stride, a second clock inside one
10242               	; 	' figure, which is the flapping of DESIGN.md 0j2 in the vertical.
10243               	; 	'
10244               	; 	' Safe because HARRY'S COLLISION HAS NO VERTICAL COMPONENT: he is caught on
10245               	; 	' `same level AND |dx| < CATCHR`. Doing this to Kelly would desynchronise
10246               	; 	' his sprite from the ball and biplane windows in DESIGN.md 5a, which is
10247               	; 	' why it is not done to him.
10248               	; 	' THE WHOLE FIGURE RISES, legs included, rather than the body stretching
10249               	; 	' away from the feet. Two reasons, and the second was not the first plan:
10250               	; 	'
10251               	; 	'   * It is what a run does. Mid-flight BOTH feet are off the ground, so
10252               	; 	'     lifting the legs with the body is the correct pose, not a compromise.
10253               	; 	'   * Lifting the body alone opens a one-pixel gap at the waist, and the
10254               	; 	'     torso's bottom row and the legs' top row do not align across it --
10255               	; 	'     green shows through and he reads as coming apart.
10256               	; 	'
10257               	; 	' It also keeps `hfy = hy - 7` and `hy2 = hy + 16` LITERAL, which is not a
10258               	; 	' cosmetic concern: assets/checkbands.py reads each band's draw offset by
10259               	; 	' parsing those exact expressions. Bobbing a differently-named variable
10260               	; 	' made them unreadable and the gate failed the build -- correctly. A
10261               	; 	' checker that can no longer see what it checks has to fail, not shrug.

10729 E9DE C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
10730 E9E0 0450  20 	b *r0
10731               	;
10732               	; 	' ======================================================================
10733               	; 	' THE SCANNER
10734               	; 	' ======================================================================
10735               	; 	' Rows 21-23, 16 chars wide, centred: 48 characters whose PATTERNS are
10736               	; 	' poked directly, so a moving dot costs two writes and no name-table
10737               	; 	' traffic. With the flip (DESIGN.md 2a) this is how you know where Harry
10738               	; 	' is at all -- he can be one pixel off-screen and completely invisible.
10739               	; 	'
10740               	; 	' Rows 21-23 all sit in the THIRD screen third, so every scanner pattern
10741               	; 	' is at base 4096 and no third-selection arithmetic is needed.
10742               	; 	'
10743               	; 	' BOTH DOTS ARE WHITE and Kelly's BLINKS. The manual wants Kelly black
10744               	; 	' and Harry white, but this is the TMS9918's two-colours-per-8x1-cell
10745               	; 	' mode: a black dot and a white dot on the same pixel row of the same
10746               	; 	' character cannot both exist. Blinking distinguishes them with one
10747               	; 	' colour, which is what RallyX does with its player dot.
10748               	; 	' THE STEPS MOVE, AND EVERY ESCALATOR ON SCREEN MOVES WITH THEM. A flight
10749               	; 	' is twelve characters -- six per direction, sliced out of a rendered
10750               	; 	' 64x40 bitmap by genart.py -- so rewriting those twelve PATTERNS animates
10751               	; 	' the lot: 96 bytes, no name-table traffic, and the same cost whether one
10752               	; 	' flight is visible or ten. The phases slide the cells ALONG the slope, so
10753               	; 	' the steps travel up the flight rather than drifting sideways.
10754               	; esc_tick:
10755               cvb_ESC_TICK
10756               	; 	' ONLY SCREENS 0 AND 7 CARRY A FLIGHT, so six screens in eight skip this
10757               	; 	' entirely -- which is what makes rewriting the cells every frame
10758               	; 	' affordable. Nested, not `klsc > 0 AND klsc < 7`: the 9900 backend
10759               	; 	' miscompiles a compare-AND-compare (CLAUDE.md 3A).
10760               	; 	IF klsc > 0 THEN
10761 E9E2 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     E9E4 217E     
10762 E9E6 0201  20 	li r1,256   ; 1*256
     E9E8 0100     
10763 E9EA 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
10764 E9EC 1402  14 	jhe cv814
10765 E9EE 0460  28 	b @cv813
     E9F0 EA06     
10766               cv814
10767               	; 		IF klsc < 7 THEN RETURN
10768 E9F2 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     E9F4 217E     
10769 E9F6 0201  20 	li r1,1792   ; 7*256
     E9F8 0700     
10770 E9FA 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
10771 E9FC 1A02  14 	jl cv816
10772 E9FE 0460  28 	b @cv815
     EA00 EA06     
10773               cv816
10774 EA02 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
10775 EA04 0450  20 	b *r0
10776               cv815
10777               	; 	END IF
10778               cv813
10779               	; 	' ONE PHASE PER PASS, AND NEVER MORE. There are four phases covering an
10780               	; 	' 8 px period, so advancing by the frame delta ALIASES: at fdv 2 the
10781               	; 	' pattern flips between two positions half a period apart and the
10782               	; 	' direction stops being readable, and at fdv 3 the sequence runs 0,3,2,1
10783               	; 	' -- the staircase visibly runs BACKWARDS, carrying its steps down while
10784               	; 	' the rider goes up. That is the wagon-wheel effect, and no amount of
10785               	; 	' phase arithmetic fixes it: a 4-phase cycle can only be stepped by 1.
10786               	; 	'
10787               	; 	' So the ANIMATION is the clock here, and the rider is paced from it --
10788               	; 	' see move_kelly, which advances him by the same fixed 2-and-1 per pass
10789               	; 	' rather than by fdv. Locking the two together is the only way they
10790               	; 	' cannot drift, and it is what "the player moves at the rate the steps
10791               	; 	' carry him" actually means. The cost is that a ride takes 36 passes
10792               	; 	' rather than 36 frames, so it slows down when the loop does -- which is
10793               	; 	' the honest behaviour for a machine that is carrying you.
10794               	; 	escp = escp + 1
10795 EA06 D020  34 	movb @cvb_ESCP,r0
     EA08 20B0     
10796 EA0A 0220  22 	ai r0,256	; 1
     EA0C 0100     
10797 EA0E D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_ESCP
     EA10 20B0     
10798               	; 	IF escp > 3 THEN escp = 0
10799 EA12 0201  20 	li r1,1024   ; 4*256
     EA14 0400

10801 EA18 1402  14 	jhe cv818
10802 EA1A 0460  28 	b @cv817
     EA1C EA24     
10803               cv818
10804 EA1E 04C0  14 	clr r0
10805 EA20 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_ESCP
     EA22 20B0     
10806               cv817
10807               	; 	' ONLY THE CELLS THAT MOVE, AND ONLY THIS SCREEN'S. genart.py measures
10808               	; 	' which cells move -- the step cells and the COMPOSITE copies of them
10809               	; 	' that cross a floor -- and groups them WEST then EAST, because only one
10810               	; 	' direction is ever on screen: screen 0 carries a west flight and screen
10811               	; 	' 7 an east one.
10812               	; 	'
10813               	; 	' THIS IS THE ONLY PER-PASS WORK UNIQUE TO THESE TWO SCREENS, and it is
10814               	; 	' `define_char`, which in bitmap mode is LDIRVM3 -- the TRIPLE copy, once
10815               	; 	' per screen third. It therefore costs three times what the character
10816               	; 	' count suggests, which is why these screens run slower than the rest.
10817               	; 	'
10818               	; 	' THE WEST SCREEN USED TO COST HALF AS MUCH AGAIN, because it carries TWO
10819               	; 	' flights: floor 1's crosses into a shop floor and floor 3's into the
10820               	; 	' roof, so it needed both composite sets -- nine characters against the
10821               	; 	' east's six, 216 bytes a pass against 144. It measured 20 passes a
10822               	; 	' second where the east screen managed 24-26. The two sets are
10823               	; 	' PIXEL-IDENTICAL and are never wanted in the same screen THIRD, so they
10824               	; 	' share their characters now and are told apart by the per-third colour
10825               	; 	' table (see esc_deck_col in setup). Six either side.
10826               	; 	'
10827               	; 	' The handrail and the frame sit past the end of both ranges and never
10828               	; 	' move at all.
10829               	; 	IF klsc = 0 THEN

11088 EC60 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
11089 EC62 0450  20 	b *r0
11090               	;
11091               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------- the scanner's furniture
11092               	; 	' Without this the scanner is two dots in a void: you can see THAT Harry is
11093               	; 	' somewhere, but not what he is near, which is the one thing you actually
11094               	; 	' need when he is off-screen. Floor lines say which level, the slashes say
11095               	; 	' where that floor's escalator is, and the bar says where the elevator is --
11096               	; 	' exactly the three things the manual lists.
11097               	; 	'
11098               	; 	' FURNITURE AND DOTS ARE SEGREGATED BY PIXEL ROW ON PURPOSE, and a level
11099               	; 	' band is now FOUR rows, one per colour, because a pixel row carries
11100               	; 	' exactly one colour here:
11101               	; 	'
11102               	; 	'   +0  GREY    escalator head, and the elevator car
11103               	; 	'   +1  GREY    the air of that floor
11104               	; 	'   +2  GREY
11105               	; 	'   +3  YELLOW  the floor line -- and NOTHING else goes here
11106               	; 	'
11107               	; 	' THE FIRST THREE ROWS ARE THE FLOOR'S AIR AND EVERYTHING LIVES IN THEM:
11108               	; 	' the escalator's three-step diagonal, the lift car, and both actors, all
11109               	; 	' three pixels tall. They are grey in the table, and the Kop and the
11110               	; 	' crook recolour their own CHARACTER as they move (scan_mark) -- which is
11111               	; 	' what lets both of them fill the band instead of getting a pixel row
11112               	; 	' each. Eight pixels of this canvas is half a screen of the store, so
11113               	; 	' they are almost never in one character; when they are, they merge.
11114               	; 	'
11115               	; 	' Shrinking from six rows bought eight empty pixel rows, four above the
11116               	; 	' instrument and four below, so it no longer butts against the shop floor
11117               	; 	' above it or the bottom of the screen.
11118               	; 	'
11119               	; 	' The dots are erased by ANDing their bits out, so anything sharing a row
11120               	; 	' with one is rubbed away wherever an actor has passed -- which is why
11121               	; 	' scan_escs and scan_elev redraw every tick rather than once.
11122               	; scan_furn:
11123               cvb_SCAN_FURN

11539 EFB6 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
11540 EFB8 0450  20 	b *r0
11541               	;
11542               	; 	' Blank the canvas by WRITING zeros rather than copying 384 of them out
11543               	; 	' of ROM. This runs twice a game -- at setup and on a new Krook -- so a
11544               	; 	' loop costs nothing anybody can perceive, and it hands the fixed area
11545               	; 	' back the best part of half a kilobyte, which is the budget that
11546               	; 	' actually binds. Paced in bursts: a few hundred VDP writes in a single
11547               	; 	' frame are silently dropped (CLAUDE.md 3A).
11548               	; scan_wipe:
11549               cvb_SCAN_WIPE
11550               	; 	' THIS ADDRESS IS A CHARACTER CODE IN DISGUISE, AND IT WENT STALE.
11551               	; 	' 4096 is the pattern table's BOTTOM THIRD -- rows 16-23, which is
11552               	; 	' band 0 and the radar together -- and the canvas characters begin at
11553               	; 	' genart's SCAN_FIRST, so the base is 4096 + SCAN_FIRST*8. It was
11554               	; 	' written out by hand as 4096 + 160*8 when SCAN_FIRST was 160; art
11555               	; 	' added later moved SCAN_FIRST to 208 and nothing moved this with it,
11556               	; 	' so the wipe zeroed the 48 characters BELOW the canvas and never
11557               	; 	' touched the canvas at all.
11558               	; 	'
11559               	; 	' Two of those forty-eight are STRUCTURE. ENDWALL is the building's
11560               	; 	' outside wall and SLABP is a pillar's cap. Blanking a pattern in ONE
11561               	; 	' THIRD leaves a character that draws correctly everywhere else and
11562               	; 	' falls back to its own background colour here -- so the GROUND FLOOR's
11563               	; 	' outside wall turned into plain green while the identical wall one
11564               	; 	' storey up was perfect, and the ground floor's pillars lost the brick
11565               	; 	' carried up through the bar above them. ENDWALL appears only on
11566               	; 	' screens 0 and 7, which is exactly why it was reported as happening
11567               	; 	' only on the escalator screens, mirrored side to side.
11568               	; 	'
11569               	; 	' The name table was correct the whole time. Probing it said so, four
11570               	; 	' times. It was the GLYPH that was empty, in one third only -- which a
11571               	; 	' probe that copies the character somewhere else to look at it cannot
11572               	; 	' see, because the copy renders from a different third's pattern.
11573               	; 	'
11574               	; 	' renumber.py rewrites this line from genart.SCAN_FIRST, and
11575               	; 	' checkstruct.py fails the build if the two disagree.
11576               	; 	#swa = 5760			' 4096 + SCAN_FIRST*8, the canvas patterns
11577 EFBA 0200  20 	li r0,5760
     EFBC 1680     
11578 EFBE C800  38 	mov r0,@cvb__SWA
     EFC0 20D8     
11579               	; 	FOR swj = 0 TO 5
11580 EFC2 04C0  14 	clr r0
11581 EFC4 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_SWJ
     EFC6 2091     
11582               cv872
11583               	; 		FOR swi = 0 TO 63
11584 EFC8 04C0  14 	clr r0

     EFCC 208F

11588 EFCE 04C0  14 	clr r0
11589 EFD0 064A  14 	dect r10

11591 EFD4 C020  34 	mov @cvb__SWA,r0

     F04C 21D5     
11647               	;
11648               	; 	' THE TWO ACTORS ARE SPRITES; EVERYTHING ELSE IS CHARACTERS.
11649               	; 	'
11650               	; 	' That split is what makes the instrument simple. The floor lines, the
11651               	; 	' flights and the lift car do not move relative to the canvas, so they
11652               	; 	' are pixels in the scanner's own characters and cost nothing to keep.
11653               	; 	' The Kop and the crook DO move, and as sprites they need no colour
11654               	; 	' table, no erase and no priority arithmetic -- they draw over the
11655               	; 	' furniture because sprites always do, and nothing shifts to make room.
11656               	; 	'
11657               	; 	' IT ALSO REMOVED THE BLINK. Kelly flashed because a pixel row of this
11658               	; 	' canvas can only carry one colour, so at close range the two markers
11659               	; 	' merged and the blink was the only thing left to tell them apart. Two
11660               	; 	' sprites are two colours wherever they stand.
11661               	; 	'
11662               	; 	' The furniture is drawn ONCE, by scan_furn. It used to be redrawn every
11663               	; 	' tick because erasing a character-based marker ANDed bits out of
11664               	; 	' whatever it was standing on; there is nothing left to rub it away. The
11665               	; 	' car still redraws itself, because the car actually moves.
11666               	; 	' -- the lift car. IT WAS FLICKERING, and the cause was not the drawing
11667               	; 	' but the ERASING: scan_elev cleared its three cells and put them back
11668               	; 	' every tick, whether or not the car had moved, so several times a second
11669               	; 	' there was a window with the pixels off. A sprite has no erase at all --
11670               	; 	' it is placed, and the VDP does the rest -- and slot 26 sits BELOW the
11671               	; 	' two markers, so the Kop and the crook pass over it without either of
11672               	; 	' them having to know the car exists.
11673               	; 	say = 3 - elvl
11674 F04E D020  34 	movb @cvb_ELVL,r0

12378               	;
12379               	; 	' THE BONUS IS COUNTED OUT, NOT AWARDED. Dropping the whole sum into the
12380               	; 	' score in one frame tells the player a number; ticking the clock down a
12381               	; 	' unit at a time, with the score climbing and a beat under each step,
12382               	; 	' tells them what the number was FOR. It is the pay-off for every second
12383               	; 	' they saved, and every arcade game of this era spends a moment on it.
12384               	; 	'
12385               	; 	' It also costs nothing to get right: the loop already has to run down
12386               	; 	' `tsec`, so the animation IS the calculation, and the repeated-addition
12387               	; 	' note below stops applying -- each step adds one band's worth.
12388               	; 	'
12389               	; 	' x100 for Krooks 1-9, x200 for 10-15, x300 from 16. Two independent
12390               	; 	' sources agree on those bands; the manual's own wording suggests
12391               	; 	' 1-8 / 9-16 / 17+ and DESIGN.md 0 records the disagreement.
12392               	; 	'
12393               	; 	' #bval MUST BE 16-BIT: 300 does not fit in a byte, and a plain variable
12394               	; 	' would silently truncate it to 44 (CLAUDE.md 3A).

     F706 F658     
12498               	;
12499               	; 	' `calc_bonus` USED TO LIVE HERE and computed the whole sum by repeated
12500               	; 	' addition, to dodge two hazards at once: `tsec * bmul` is an 8-bit
12501               	; 	' product (a 50-unit capture in the x300 band pays 15,000, six times past
12502               	; 	' a byte), and multiplying properly lands on the MPY/r0 hazard where a
12503               	; 	' 16-bit variable read straight after being multiplied returns the
12504               	; 	' product's high word. Counting the bonus out a unit at a time (above)
12505               	; 	' removes the multiply entirely rather than working around it -- the loop
12506               	; 	' the player watches IS the arithmetic.
12507               	; 	'
12508               	; 	' It also fixed the last-tick case for free: `bn_loop` tests `tsec = 0`
12509               	; 	' on entry, where the old `FOR bi = 1 TO tsec` would have run its body
12510               	; 	' once and paid a full multiplier for no time at all (CLAUDE.md 3A).
12511               	;
12512               	; do_escape:
12513               cvb_DO_ESCAPE
12514               	; 	escapd = 0

     F836 AA16     
12646               	;
12647               	; 	' Silence every channel FIRST. SOUND latches, and the per-channel decay
12648               	; 	' counters are not ticked inside this loop -- a tone still sounding when
12649               	; 	' the pause starts would hang for its whole duration, which is the classic
12650               	; 	' CVBasic sticky-audio failure.
12651               	; 	' EVERY CHANNEL OFF, AND THE DECAY COUNTERS WITH THEM. The counters are
12652               	; 	' ticked by the main loop, so anything still sounding when the loop stops
12653               	; 	' -- for a capture, a death, the bonus count -- sustains for as long as
12654               	; 	' whatever replaced the loop takes. That is where the long beep over the
12655               	; 	' bonus tally came from: not the tally's own note, which turns itself off
12656               	; 	' after every unit, but a footstep or a hit that was still ringing when
12657               	; 	' Harry was caught.
12658               	; snd_off:
12659               cvb_SND_OFF
12660               	; 	SOUND 0,0,0
12661 F838 04C0  14 	clr r0
12662 F83A 0202  20 	li r2,>8000
     F83C 8000

     F840 A980

12699               	; 	' AND THE EFFECTS THAT HAVE NOT HAPPENED YET. A set sf* flag is a sound
12700               	; 	' waiting for the next pass of the main loop -- and between a capture and
12701               	; 	' the next Krook the main loop does not run, so anything latched during
12702               	; 	' the round (a hit as Harry was caught, the bonus Kop the tally just
12703               	; 	' awarded) survived the silence and fired on the first pass of the NEW
12704               	; 	' round. Silencing the channels does not help: the flag plays a fresh
12705               	; 	' note afterwards, with a fresh decay counter, which is why it came out
12706               	; 	' as one long tone over the start of a level that had earned nothing.

12757               	; 	' ======================================================================
12758               	; 	' SOUND
12759               	; 	' ======================================================================
12760               	; 	' Every effect gets an explicit note-off. SOUND latches -- with no
12761               	; 	' `SOUND ch,f,0` the last tone sustains for ever. And the second argument
12762               	; 	' is a 10-BIT DIVISOR, max 1023: SMALLER IS HIGHER, so a rising sweep is
12763               	; 	' written as a falling number and anything over 1023 is silently masked
12764               	; 	' to an unrelated pitch.

     FBB2 FBCC     
13070               cv990
13071 FBB4 04C0  14 	clr r0
13072 FBB6 0202  20 	li r2,>a000
     FBB8 A000     
13073 FBBA 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     FBBC A980     
13074 FBBE A2BA     	data sn76489_freq
13075 FBC0 04C0  14 	clr r0
13076 FBC2 0202  20 	li r2,>b000
     FBC4 B000     
13077 FBC6 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     FBC8 A980     
13078 FBCA A2D6     	data sn76489_vol
13079               cv989
13080               	; 	END IF
13081               cv987
13082               	; 	IF spt > 0 THEN
13083 FBCC D020  34 	movb @cvb_SPT,r0
     FBCE 2206     
13084 FBD0 0201  20 	li r1,256   ; 1*256
     FBD2 0100     
13085 FBD4 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
13086 FBD6 1402  14 	jhe cv992
13087 FBD8 0460  28 	b @cv991
     FBDA FC0A     
13088               cv992
13089               	; 		spt = spt - 1
13090 FBDC D020  34 	movb @cvb_SPT,r0
     FBDE 2206     
13091 FBE0 0220  22 	ai r0,65280	; -1
     FBE2 FF00     
13092 FBE4 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_SPT
     FBE6 2206     
13093               	; 		IF spt = 0 THEN SOUND 2,0,0
13094 FBE8 04C1  14 	clr r1
13095 FBEA 9001  18 	cb r1,r0
13096 FBEC 1302  14 	jeq cv994
13097 FBEE 0460  28 	b @cv993
     FBF0 FC0A     
13098               cv994
13099 FBF2 04C0  14 	clr r0
13100 FBF4 0202  20 	li r2,>c000
     FBF6 C000     
13101 FBF8 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     FBFA A980     
13102 FBFC A2BA     	data sn76489_freq
13103 FBFE 04C0  14 	clr r0
13104 FC00 0202  20 	li r2,>d000
     FC02 D000     
13105 FC04 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     FC06 A980     
13106 FC08 A2D6     	data sn76489_vol
13107               cv993
13108               	; 	END IF
13109               cv991
13110               	; 	RETURN
13111 FC0A C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
13112 FC0C 0450  20 	b *r0
13113               	;
13114               	; 	' EVERYTHING BELOW THIS LINE IS ASSEMBLED INTO BANK 1, so the INCLUDE order
13115               	; 	' is load-bearing and nothing but data may follow it.
13116               	; 	'
13117               	; 	' THE FONT IS NOW IN THE BANK TOO, and it used to be deliberately outside:
13118               	; 	' keeping one readable thing in bank 0 means a bank-selection mistake shows
13119               	; 	' as "text survives, art does not" rather than a uniformly blank screen,
13120               	; 	' which is a genuinely useful thing to have when a bank goes wrong.
13121               	; 	'
13122               	; 	' It was given up for 472 bytes, because the fixed area had 342 left and
13123               	; 	' that diagnostic is worth less than the ability to keep building. Two
13124               	; 	' things make it a fair trade rather than a straight loss:
13125               	; 	'
13126               	; 	'   * `BANK SELECT 1` already runs BEFORE `DEFINE CHAR 32,59,font_bits`
13127               	; 	'     (see setup), so nothing about the read order had to change. If that
13128               	; 	'     ever stops being true the font goes blank at boot, immediately and
13129               	; 	'     unmistakably -- a loud failure, not a subtle one.
13130               	; 	'   * The alternative on the table was banking the 29 PRINT AT literals,
13131               	; 	'     which recovers LESS (about 360 bytes net after the reader routine)
13132               	; 	'     and would blind assets/checklayout.py, whose whole method is parsing
13133               	; 	'     `PRINT AT n,"literal"` to catch a string running past column 31 or
13134               	; 	'     a HUD poke landing inside a label. Trading a diagnostic for bytes is
13135               	; 	'     one thing; trading a build gate for fewer bytes is another.
13136               	; 	#if TI994A
13137               	; 	BANK 1
13138      03F0     BANK_0_FREE:	EQU >fffe-$
     **** ****     > .rept

13276               	; 	INCLUDE "art.bas"
13277               	; 	' Keystone Kapers art -- GENERATED by assets/genart.py. Do not edit; regenerate.
13278               	; 	'
13279               	; 	' EVERY SPRITE IS 16x16: sprite size and magnification are GLOBAL on the
13280               	; 	' TMS9918, so small objects are drawn small INSIDE a 16x16 box rather than
13281               	; 	' being small sprites.
13282               	; 	'
13283               	; 	' EVERY 8-PX OBJECT SITS IN ROWS 8-15 OF ITS BOX, ducked Kelly included, so
13284               	; 	' one rule places them all: bottom edge = y + 16, and a thing resting on the
13285               	; 	' floor is at y = FLOORY - 16.
13286               	; 	'
13287               	; 	' Sprite bytes are QUADRANT-ORDERED: 16 rows of the left half, then 16 of
13288               	; 	' the right. Every block is an EVEN number of bytes -- an odd DATA BYTE run
13289               	; 	' silently misaligns every word table after it (CLAUDE.md 3A).
13290               	;
13291               	; spr_kelly:	' Kelly: RIGHT hat/face/tunic/tunic-arm-up/duck-hat/duck-face, then the same LEFT (+24), then the FOUR shared run frames. Patterns 0..60
13292               cvb_SPR_KELLY
13293               	; 	DATA BYTE $00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00

14296               	;
14297               	; 	' -------------------------------------------------- scanner canvas
14298               	; 	' Chars 208-255: 16 cols x 3 rows over screen rows 21-23. Blank to start;
14299               	; 	' dots are plotted by VPOKEing the PATTERN table (rows 21-23 sit in the
14300               	; 	' third screen third, so every scanner pattern is at base 4096).
14301               	;
14302               	; 	' --------------------------------------------------- font colours
14303               	; 	' Chars 32-90, EIGHT bytes each. Without this the font keeps whatever
14304               	; 	' CVBasic left in the colour table, which on a green store made the HUD
14305               	; 	' unreadable.
14306               	;
14307               	; scan_col3:	' one 8-byte block per canvas row: 4 px of GREY margin, then a 3 px band of furniture GREY and a yellow floor line, per 4 px level, on dark green. The Kop and the crook are coloured PER CHARACTER at run time -- see scan_set
14308               cvb_SCAN_COL3
14309               	; 	DATA BYTE $EE,$EE,$EE,$EE,$EC,$EC,$EC,$BC
14310 70D2 EEEE     	byte >ee,>ee,>ee,>ee,>ec,>ec,>ec,>bc
     70D4 EEEE     
     70D6 ECEC     
     70D8 ECBC     
14311               	; 	DATA BYTE $EC,$EC,$EC,$BC,$EC,$EC,$EC,$BC
14312 70DA ECEC     	byte >ec,>ec,>ec,>bc,>ec,>ec,>ec,>bc
     70DC ECBC     
     70DE ECEC     
     70E0 ECBC     
14313               	; 	DATA BYTE $EC,$EC,$EC,$BC,$EE,$EE,$EE,$EE
14314 70E2 ECEC     	byte >ec,>ec,>ec,>bc,>ee,>ee,>ee,>ee
     70E4 ECBC     
     70E6 EEEE     
     70E8 EEEE     
14315               	;
14316               	; esc_deck:	' 6 x (char, 8 colour bytes) -- write into the THIRD-0 colour table at 8192 + char*8, once, at setup
14317               cvb_ESC_DECK

14421               	; 	INCLUDE "store.bas"
14422               	; 	' Keystone Kapers store -- GENERATED by assets/genstore.py. Do not edit.
14423               	; 	'
14424               	; 	' EIGHT band templates of 5 rows x 32 cols (160 bytes each) plus a 32-byte
14425               	; 	' index over (level, screen). The whole 8-screen, 4-level building is about
14426               	; 	' 1.3 KB instead of the 5,120 bytes it would cost to store literally.
14427               	; 	'
14428               	; 	' All eight templates are ONE contiguous block under ONE label, so a band
14429               	; 	' blit is
14430               	; 	'     SCREEN stor_tpl, tpl*160, toprow*32, 32, 5, 32
14431               	; 	' with no label ladder -- SCREEN needs a literal label and a label cannot be
14432               	; 	' chosen by a variable, which is why RallyX needed a 4-way IF over its maze
14433               	; 	' tables. One block sidesteps it.
14434               	; 	'
14435               	; 	' 160 is EVEN, so this cannot leave the location counter odd and misalign
14436               	; 	' the word tables defined after it.
14437               	; 	'
14438               	; 	' template ids:
14439               	; 	'   0 T_AISLE_A
14440               	; 	'   1 T_AISLE_B
14441               	; 	'   2 T_ESC_W
14442               	; 	'   3 T_ESC_E
14443               	; 	'   4 T_ELEV
14444               	; 	'   5 T_ROOF
14445               	; 	'   6 T_ROOF_W
14446               	; 	'   7 T_ROOF_E
14447               	; 	'   8 T_END_W
14448               	; 	'   9 T_END_E
14449               	;
14450               	; stor_tpl:	' 10 templates x 160 bytes
14451               cvb_STOR_TPL
